1.0 Introduction
1.1 Document Charter
This document describes the data model that comprises the dataPOWER/
dataBASE package. 
This document includes:
• Data flow information: 
• dataBASE tables and relationships.
• What conventions / standardizations exist at the data sources.
• Data dictionary information:
• What are legal values for some data fields which need standardization 
across the implementation scope of dataPOWER.
• What usage is made of the data, both in the interactive dataPOWER tool 
and in regular reports.
• Default values for those data fields that have defaults.
• Definitions of terms used (“Glossary,” page 517).
1.2 Supporting Documents
This document is one part of the complete documentation set that should be 
accessible to dataPOWER administrators and end-users. The following documents 
comprise the list of supporting documentation for the dataPOWER system:
General dataPOWER OnLine Documentation Library — The dataPOWER library 
of on-line documents, including all dataPOWER reference documents listed here. 
This library is delivered in Adobe Acrobat™ PDF™ format. 
Release Notes — dataPOWER 8.x — Database administrator and 
dataPOWER user notes for dataPOWER release 8.0 and higher.
End-Users dataPOWER End-Users Manual — Reference manual for the core 
dataPOWER product, including:
• dataPOWER Environment — working with dataPOWER worksheets, manip￾ulating data, dataPOWER menus and palettes
• Database Retrieval — standard dataBASE data retrieval window functional￾ity, including Raw, Statistics, Multi-Program, Metrology and Equipment His￾tory
• dataPOWER Charts — statistical analysis and chart generation tools, includ￾ing: ANOVA, Histograms, Boxplots, Product Sensitivity Analysis, Regression, 
Reject Oriented Analysis
• dataPOWER Reports — dataPOWER analysis reports, including: Custom 
Summary Report, Extreme Value Reports, Temperature Characterization
• Yield Optimization Tools — yieldOPTIMIZER Database Trend Analysis 
tools, including Yield Report and Zonal Report

# Page 16

dataPOWER
16
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
• Journal — tools that enable you to create presentation quality documents from 
dataPOWER, in PowerPoint or Microsoft Word format.
dataPOWER Mapping Tools Users Manual — The reference manual for all 
dataPOWER mapping tools:
• defectMAP — defect analysis and defect/wafer mapping
• waferMAP/binMAP — parametric wafer and bin mapping
• bitMAP — bit failure wafer-based mapping
dataPOWER Data Mining Tools Users Manual — 
• Classification Trees — used for the development of classification trees 
intended to suggest possible reasons for the existence of groups, clusters, or 
“classes” of events.
• Self Organizing Maps — the SOM tool provides a visual overview of high 
dimensional data sets to help you explore the data and identify groups within 
it. 
• FSS and FSDA — Wafer Signature Analysis Reports. The FSS (Fail Signature 
Summary) tool is designed for faster, larger analyses of lots for predetermined 
wafer signatures. The FSDA (Fail Signature Detection and Analysis) tool is 
designed for more detailed analysis of lots, using algorithms that automatically 
identify and sort significant wafer signatures.
Administrators dataPOWER Products Installation and Configuration Guide — 
Informix or Oracle Database — The administrators guide to the installation 
and configuration manual of dataPOWER and related software products, for 
installations using the Informix or Oracle database engine.
dataBASE Administrator Utilities Manual — The reference manual for 
dataBASE Administrators, defining usage of tools for database configuration, data 
exporting, lot statistics updating, batch scheduling and administration. All 
administration operations windows, such as Program, Lot, Parameter, Product, 
Zonal Operations, Tagging, database parameter scaling, database privileges, 
dataFLOW, etc. are also documented.
dataPOWER Data Dictionary — The schema document for the data model 
and data tables that make up the dataPOWER database.
Data Readers – dataPOWER — The reference manual for the dataPOWER 
database and worksheet reader configurations, providing both an end-user’s 
overview and a functional description of the formatting and configuration for 
individual readers (ASCII, Metrology, bitMAP reader, etc.).
Training dataPOWER Training and Tutorial Guide — Intended for first-time users 
and users who want to learn new areas of the dataPOWER tool set. The modules 
in this guide have been constructed to introduce database retrieval techniques, data 
manipulation, and the most widely used dataPOWER analysis charts and tools. 
These procedures are used with the dataPOWER training database and a pre￾generated set of example worksheets.

dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
dataPOWER Administrator Training — Intended for dataPOWER 
administrators and DBAs. Provides hands-on training for most dataPOWER 
administrator tools, including database operations windows, data readers, data 
retrieval, license management, database schema, database platform and data 
export. This manual is primarily intended for in-class use, but can also serve as a 
stand-alone training tool.
Developers HyperScript Programming Manual – dataPOWER Application 
Programming Interface — The developer’s guide to the dataPOWER API 
environment and statistics engine. This manual is intended for programmers or 
“power users” who will be developing or customizing system features.
HyperScript Programming Manual – Commands — The developer’s 
guide to the syntax and definition of each HyperScript command.
HyperScript Programming Manual – Functions — The developer’s 
guide to the components of the HyperScript functions that are used in 
dataPOWER.

FLEXlm FLEXlm End User Manual — the user manual for FLEXlm, the licence 
manager software used with dataPOWER products.

CONTEXT SENSITIVE HTML HELP SYSTEM
The dataPOWER context-sensitive HTML-based Help system works with your 
default browser and is launched in two ways: from the menu or from most of the 
windows and dialogs in dataPOWER.
• From the menu, select the Help > dataPOWER Online Help menu function

 1.0 — Introduction 19
dataPOWER Data Dictionary

to access the top-level Help GUI.
• From most windows and dialogs, you can click on the button in the chart 
or tool you are using and the documentation topic for the current interface/

dataPOWER Data Dictionary

chart will be opened in a new HTML window.
Not every manual in the dataPOWER documentation library are included. The 
documents published in this on-line Help system are:
• dataPOWER End-User Manual
• Mapping Tools End-User Manual
• Data Mining User Manual
• dataPOWER Release Notes
The system is compatible with any current web browser and includes intelligent 
full-text search and a keyword index.
The full dataPOWER documentation set, including all “back-end” documentation 
is also published in Acrobat PDF form, as described previously. This includes the 
manuals published in the Help system format. These documents are launched from 
the Help > dataPOWER Manuals submenu.

Search The Search panel lets you search the text of the entire Help system for a topic. The

topic contains the search term, the topic will appear at the top of the results list.

ON-LINE PDF DOCUMENTATION AND GLOBAL DOCUMENT
INDEXING INSTALLATION
Delete Old 
On-Line 
Documentation
If you already have older Acrobat documents installed 
for a previous dataPOWER software release, you must 
delete or move the entire contents of the obsolete 
directories and subdirectories before installing the new 
documentation set. If you are replacing existing files, 
this documentation directory should be set up as the 
…\PDF\dp\dpdocs directory in your dataPOWER 
installation directory.
Delete all of the ….pdf files, the index.pdx file and the 
index directory. Do not delete the help directory; it is 
for the separate HTML Help system.
Installing New 
On-Line 
Documentation
After deleting any older versions of dataPOWER Docs Online:
1. Copy the entire contents of the Docs directory from your installation 
package or your documentation update package to an appropriate location on 
your workstation or server. If you install locally, you need to copy the entire 
directory contents into the …\PDF\dp\dpdocs directory. When the 
document set is updated for new releases, the contents of this directory 
always needs to be deleted and replaced with the newest version of document 
set.
2. If not already installed, install Acrobat Reader. (Version 9.0 or higher is 
preferred, and the following instructions will apply to version 9. But earlier 
versions will work in much the same way as described here and the files will 
be compatible.) You can download the Acrobat software from 
http://get.adobe.com/uk/reader/.
3. Open the idslibrary.pdf file for a top-level menu of all manuals, linking to 
all of the documents in the set.
Setting Up the 
dataPOWER
Search Library 
Index

Reader, by adding the dataPOWER index.pdx index file in your Reader set-up. 
This section demonstrates how to select the index.pdx index in Acrobat Reader 
version 9x. This operation only needs to be performed once for each client PC to 
update your system so it will search the new document set.
1. Open Acrobat Reader.

Using Acrobat to 
Search Multiple 
Documents
If you only need to search only your currently opened document, simply open the 
file and use the Find field in the Acrobat tool bar. But if you need to search the 
entire dataPOWER document set, you’ll use the advanced search options. With an 
up-to-date dataPOWER Acrobat search index installed (as described above), you 
can search the entire dataPOWER library and find a term or text string in any 
dataPOWER document.

panel, select the Use Advanced

1.3 Conventions and Usage
The following conventions exist throughout the document:
• Variables (typically lower case) and table names (typically upper case) are 
printed in sans serif font.
• For online document users:
• Any term appearing in blue text is hyperlinked to a related section, e.g.
Conventions and Usage.
• Any term appearing in pink text is hyperlinked to its glossary definition, e.g. 
program.
• n → 1 and 1 → n links between tables are described/listed at the “n” and “1” side 
of the link.
• n → m links between tables A and B are implemented in the data model by 
means of a hidden table (A2B) between them. The hidden table will be listed in 
references to the link.

2.0 Oracle Database — More Than 1,000 Parameters 
Server Side 
Changes
dataPOWER installations on the Oracle database platform handle programs with 
more than 1,000 columns by creating additional versions of the RES…, WAF…, 
LIM…, and LOT… dynamic database tables, when necessary.
The dynamic tables on the Oracle platform limit the t1-tn test index fields to 
t[900-indexes] (n, as defined in test_index in the DEF… table.) When a program 
has more than 900 parameters, the RES…_1, WAF…_1, LIM…_1, and LOT…_1 
dynamic database tables will be created to handle the additional parameters — 901-
1,800. If there are more than 1,800 parameters, the RES…_2, WAF…_2, 
LIM…_2, and LOT…_2 dynamic database tables will be created to handle param￾eters 1,801-2,700, and so on.
Updating Old 
Databases to 6.5 
and Later
Because the Oracle dynamic tables that were limited to 999 parameters are now 
limited to 900, if an old database has between 901 and 999 parameters, running 
DbUpdate on the old database will automatically create the RES…_1, WAF…_1, 
LIM…_1, and LOT…_1 dynamic database tables to handle the additional param￾eters.

 3.0 — Log Tables 33

3.0 Log Tables
Abstract Log tables (EVENT_LOG, OP_LOG, WF_LOG, TAG_LOG, LEH_LOG, 
Z_LOG, MEM_LOG, DF_LOG and BIN_LOG) keep track of ongoing operations 
at the data sources. They form the heart of the ‘living’ part of the database. The 
BIN_LOG, TAG_LOG, DF_LOG, MEM_LOG, Z_LOG, EVENT_LOG tables are 
not described in this section; however they are described in the following sections 
“BIN_LOG,” page 79, “TAG_LOG,” page 130, “DF_LOG,” page 159, 
“MEM_LOG,” page 301, “Z_LOG,” page 154 and “EVENT_LOG,” page 440.
Along the manufacturing process of a final product, it is subjected to multiple 
process steps (PROC_STEP). A process step may either be a PCM, Esort or F/T 
test program, in which case the data insertion results in an entry in OP_LOG (and 
potentially several entries in WF_LOG) and when binning information is 
available in entries in BIN_LOG. Equipment and/or lot maintenance are handled 
as events in EVENT_LOG and linked to what is affected by the event process 
step, lot, wafer, etc. A defect inspection step results in entries in the OP_LOG and 
DF_LOG tables. A bitMAP inspection step results in entries in the MEM_LOG 
table.

4.0 PROGRAM and Related Tables
Abstract The term “program” is central in the design of dataPOWER. Each type of a 
program generates an entry in the PROGRAM table and instantiates a collection of 
five tables: RES…, WAF…, LOT…, LIM… and DEF… where the dots (‘…’) 
indicate that the table names are postfixed by pg_key. This concept is obviously 
highly denormalized for performance reasons.
Additional tables such as BIN_LOG, HIST_BIN and CONDITION are linked to 
PROGRAM to store binning and condition information.
Definition of a “program” as a record in this table is as follows: A program 
defines a set of parametric tests which is stable over time and uses a constant 
set of test limits.
Each program is associated with a fixed set of dataPOWER indexes and 
conditions that can not be changed and is automatically generated by the data 
reader, according to a given format file.
4.1 Data Model Description
Since this part of the data model is highly denormalized (for performance reasons 
— the bulk of the data is stored in the RES… tables) and is closely linked to the 
spreadsheet structure of the analysis front end, it is worthwhile to further explain 
the setup of the tables involved.
In essence, the RES… table corresponds to the data view in the dataPOWER 
analysis spreadsheet, whereas the sum (summary) view corresponds to the 
information stored in the WAF…, and LOT… tables. The lim view is a 
representation of the LIM… table.This is further explained in the following 
figures:
FIGURE 2. data view in the dataPOWER analysis spreadsheet
In the above example, the RES… table has three indexes (i0 … im-1 with m=3): 
Lot, Wafer and Device. They all happen to be key indexes; the combination of all

The table also contains 50 columns t1 … tn with n=50, corresponding to the 50 
tests executed on each device. These columns are headed by three conditions: 
Test_Name, Pin and Unit. The first two are key conditions, as only a combination 
of the two uniquely identify a column. Unit has only a descriptive purpose and is 
therefore not considered a key condition. Details about indexes are stored in 
DEF… and details of conditions in CONDITION.

# Page 46

dataPOWER
46
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
Given the above table layout, it becomes clear that a new record entered into 
PROGRAM - which causes the instantiation of a set of the previously described 
tables - should describe a raw data source, which over time will:
• Always produce the same set of tests (t1 … tm). (Or at least a reasonably big 
subset. Missing tests are stored as empty fields and thus add to the storage inef￾ficiency.)
• Always use the same test limits, as they are stored on a per program basis. This 
must be kept in mind when the program name PPID is generated, which causes a 
new entry in PROGRAM. The PPID could encode such items as:
• generic device type name
• ROM code version
• test stage
• test program revision and update level
A given entry PROGRAM can be either of prog_type fixed, semi_dynamic, 
dynamic or update rows. In the static case (default), the format of previously 
generated tables RES…, etc. will never change. Thus, if for some reason, a future 
datalog file contains additional tests, they will be neglected. Semi_Dynamic is 
similar to static, but in addition the data readers automatically fill in fields in 
tables pointed to by OP_LOG, if available in the datalog files. Dynamic programs, 
in contrast, will automatically expand the tables to handle the additional tests. 
This however, is a computationally expensive operation.
Note that such strategies as datalog on fail lead to very 
inefficient data storage, as a new record is generated in 
RES… for each failing device with only one non-empty field, 
namely for the failing test.
4.2 Tester Summary Sheet Storage
Some test systems produce both datalog and tester summary sheet data, which in 
an aggregated stage belongs to the WAF… and LOT… tables. But this is not 
feasible for the following reasons:
• Summary sheets typically have a different resolution than datalog files. E.g., 
leakage currents are measured on all pins, thus causing multiple entries (col￾umns) in RES…, but result in only one entry in the tester summary sheet.
• New releases of test programs very often have different test limits, thus must 
cause a new entry in PROGRAM and consequently instantiate a new set of 
tables (including LIM…). Only rarely do new releases contain additional tests. 
As a result, tester summary sheet information is typically stored as independent 
entries in PROGRAM. Corresponding PPID names will end with …_SUM. Since 
summary sheet formats do not typically change between test program updates, 
PPID names do need to encode test program version and update level. For 
summaries, the prog_type field in the PROGRAM table should be set to 
Dynamic.

 4.0 — PROGRAM and Related Tables 51
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
Serial Key pg_key uniquely identifies an entry in PROGRAM. It is used to 
postfix table names of RES…, WAF…, LOT…, LIM… and 
DEF…. pg_key is automatically generated out of the 
program name PPID.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
eqt_key Link into EQUIP_TYPE.
wmap_key Link into WMAP_CONFIG.
pg_grp_key Foreign key from the PROGRAM_GROUP table. If 
pr_grp_key is not NULL, program belongs to a given 
program group.
meta_key Foreign key from META_TYPES table.
identifier_key For fab/metrology programs, used to identify the step of 
the program. For WEH and LEH programs, used to 
identify the process of the program. For ASCII programs, 
used to identify the product of the program.
Other Fields in 
PROGRAM
PPID Production Program ID. This is more than the actual 
program name as, based on the PPID, a unique pg_key is 
calculated, which in turn causes instantiations of RES…
and related tables. (See “Data Model Description,” page 
44).
A typical value for PPID (assuming a family test program 
for multiple subtypes, ROM code alternatives and test 
stages) is a concatenation:
PPID = TP_name + revision (+ sub_type) (+ 
rom_code) + test_stage
pgc_key Program class key. Defines a specific grouping of 
programs. Can be set as a command line argument to the 
readers when program is new. 
prog_desc Descriptive text per program.
bins Number of bins produced by the program. This is used to 
interpret data in BIN_LOG. 
tests Number of indexes plus tests executed by the program, 
thus defining the value of n in the fields t1 … tn-indexes 
in RES…, WAF…, LOT…, LIM…Mandatory; 
automatically generated upon record insertion, by the 
data readers.
For Events, this number does not include the hidden 
indexes of the program. It only includes the parameters 
and the indexes stored in RES...

dataPOWER
54
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
bin_index Index number (starting with 0) in RES… that has 
Binning information. (lot index for LEH reader.)
tag_action Used to enable and disable the automatic tagging of 
wafers and lots. The tagging is based on the calculated 
yield and the bad_yield and scrap_yield values. 
Legal values are:
The default is 2.
For bitMAP programs, this field determines the amount 
of bitMAP raw data inserted into the database:
The default is 0.
For Events, there is no automatic tagging. This column 
specifies whether manual tagging is enabled in this 
program.
bad_yield *User-defined value from 0 to 100. It is used to tag the 
wafers and lots as “bad” when the yield is less than or 
equal to this value. When the value is less than 0, it is 
ignored and considered undefined. 
For Defect programs, this column holds the maximum 
defect density before a wafer is tagged as “High Defect 
Density” (tag_flag=5). 
For bitMAP programs, this column defines the maximum 
number of failing bits allowed per log entry. This value 
will be ignored if tag_action = 0. The default is -1.
Value Description
1 tagging is disabled
2 tagging is enabled
Value Description
0 no control
1 control using max fail
2 control using incident count
3 control using both fail count 
and incident count
Value Description
1 manual tagging is allowed
2 manual tagging is not allowed 

# Page 55

 4.0 — PROGRAM and Related Tables 55
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
scrap_yield *User-defined value from 0 to 100. It is used to tag the 
wafers and lots as “scrap” when the yield is less than or 
equal to this value. When the value is less than 0, it is 
ignored and considered undefined. 
For Defect programs, this column holds the maximum 
number of defects before a wafer is tagged as “High 
Defect Count” (tag_flag = 6)
For bitMAP programs, this column defines the maximum 
number of incidents allowed per log entry. This value 
will be ignored if tag_action = 0.
The default is -1.
*When both bad_yield and scrap_yield are defined, the 
value of scrap_yield must be less than the value of 
bad_yield.
prog_wrap A flag indicating if a program is wrapped. If it is 
wrapped, the value is set to the maximum number of 
columns. If not it is set to -1.
die_index For future use. Die traceability — die level. Default = 1.
mdl_index For future use. Die traceability — module level. 
Default = 1.
wfg_index For future use. Die traceability — wafer group level. 
Default = 1.

• TL — Tooling 
• PE — Processing Equipment
• OP — Operator
• RP — Recipe
• LT — Current Lotid
• WN — Wafer Number (current slot)
• RF — Rework 
• LO — Location
• PR — Process

Abstract Contains descriptive data as per parameter collected by a given program. The 
table contains one record per test executed (parameter collected) by the program.

by pg_key.

columns i0 … im-1 and t1 … tn respectively in RES….

for test columns, not for indexes.

Generated by the reader.
hist_stdev Historical standard deviation of test described in current 
record. Generated by the reader.
hist_count Number of entries used to calculate hist_mean and 
hist_count. Generated by the reader.

For Events class data, c1 and c5 are used according to the 
following:

dataPOWER Data Dictionary

hilim, lolim High / low acceptance limit. Data points above / below 
this limit are not inserted into the database. These can be 
entered numbers in dataPOWER or calculated using 
historical sigmas or quartiles from DEF….
lolim defaults to -1e+19 and hilim defaults to +1e+19.
The index description rows (the first n rows, where n is 
the number of indexes) use hilim to indicate the size (in 
bytes) of the data stored in that index).
That this acceptance limit is similar in effect to the outlier limits 
in statistical terms, but the effect is irreversible and rejected 
values will never be inserted into the database.
cond0 … condn Condition values (e.g. Test names, Test numbers, units, 
etc.). 
For example, for a condition named “Test Name,” a legal 
value could be “Leakage Test;” for a condition named 
“Pin,” a value would be “15” and for a condition named 
“Unit,” a value would be “mA.”

mean + nsigma mean - nsigma

Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
lg_key Foreign key into OP_LOG. lg_key is a predefined 
compulsory key index. 
ev_key Foreign key into EVENT_LOG. ev_key is a predefined 
compulsory key index.
i0 … im-1 Indexes into the table as defined by the (configuration of 
the) data reader. Typically, m is a small number and i0 … 
im-1 include device number, wafer number, etc.
For LotEvent program(15) and EquipEvent program (16), 
RES... can have one or more links to EV_... tables depending 
on the choice of the user. Whenever the user normalizes an 
index (i's) in RES..., an EV_... table is created for that index 
and a link to RES... is established. The 'x' in (ix) at the link 
represents the number of the index at RES...
Other Fields in 
RES…
t1 … tn Parametric data related to the test described by test 
indexes 1 to n (as defined in test_index in DEF…).

marketing variants of the same die type if so desired.

# Page 93

 6.0 — Product, Lot, Wafer and Related Tables 93
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
Serial Key pd_key Automatically generated, unique key for each product, 
based on pd_name.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
rs_key Each product is related to a unique RETICLE_SET. The 
fact that this link is n → 1 allows maintenance of multiple 
products per reticle set - such as marketing variants.
wmap_key Link into wafer layout definition tables WMAP_CONFIG
(and indirectly WMAPS).
test_engr Links into PEOPLE to identify responsible test engineer 
per product. 
prod_engr Links into PEOPLE to identify responsible product 
engineer per product.
apps_engr Links into PEOPLE to identify responsible applications 
engineer per product.
designer Links into PEOPLE to identify responsible designer per 
product.
user1 Links into PEOPLE to identify one user of the product.
user2 Links into PEOPLE to identify a second user of the per 
product.
Note that a product does not directly relate to a 
DEPARTMENT or FAB other than through PEOPLE. (As an 
example, user2 can be used as a mandatory link while the 
other fields are optional).
(PKG) This link supports the fact that each product (die type) 
may be packaged in different packages.
(FAMILY) This link groups products into families. The fact that 
there is an n-> m link allows flexible grouping of 
products into multiple families for different selection 
requirements.
Other Fields in 
PRODUCT
pd_name Product name as used in selection windows.
pd_desc Descriptive text per product, up to 128 characters.
code Product code.
release Release date of a the product.
die_count Number of die on wafer. This field is only valid until 
Esort.

# Page 98

dataPOWER
98
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
row_offset Number of rows offset from the first mask on the wafer.
col_offset Number of columns offset from the first mask on the 
wafer.
std_cfg A value other than N indicates that alignment between 
binmap and defect data has not been performed. Default 
is N.
Defect reader will fill with D, binmap reader will fill with 
B, DbTools will fill with U. 
die_x_offset Let DieOrigin be the X coordinate of the lower left 
corner of die (0,0) in sample coordinate system, and let 
SampleCenterLocationX be the X center of the sample 
(wafer) in the sample coordinate system, then center_x = 
DieOrigin - SampleCenterLocationX.
die_y_offset Let DieOrigin be the Y coordinate of the lower left 
corner of die (0,0) in sample coordinate system, and let 
SampleCenterLocationY be the Y center of the sample 
(wafer) in the sample coordinate system, then center_y = 
DieOrigin - SampleCenterLocationY.

6.7 WMAPS
Abstract Describes each die on a wafer map configuration. Global parameters are stored in 
WMAP_CONFIG.
Each die has an x/y (column/row) location on the wafer, which is also part of a 
stepper field. Relative to this stepper field, a die also has a column/row location.
A stepper field, in turn, has an x/y (column/row) location on the wafer, which 
corresponds to a site address.
Table
Serial Key None.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
wmap_key Index into WMAPS. This is the same as the index to 
WMAP_CONFIG. An n → 1 relation is formed by the fact 
that each record in WMAP_CONFIG has one record per 
die on wafer in WMAPS.
Other Fields in 
WMAPS
die_x, die_y Die location on the wafer.
die_type Die type is either good (normal device), bad (such as a 
device which overlaps with a PCM location) or ugly 
(such as a partial die at the wafer edge).
fld_x, fld_y Stepper field location on wafer.
fld_num Stepper field number (site). fld_num is a function of 
fld_x and fld_y.
fld_col, fld_row Die location within the stepper field.
WMAPS

die_x
die_y

fld_row

6.16 UPDATE_STATS
Abstract When a data reader (dbascii, stdf3, …etc.) processes data for a lot which already 
exists in the database, it will not recalculate the lot statistics (LOT…). Instead, it 
inserts a row in UPDATE_STATS to indicate which lots require updating. The 
UpStat utility recalculates the lot statistics and updates the appropriate LOT… 
table.
Table
Serial Key None.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
pg_key The key of the PROGRAM to which the lot belongs.
lot_key The key to the LOT that requires statistics recalculation.
wf_key The key to the WAFER that requires statistics 
recalculation. At lot level, it is set to -1.
Other Fields in 
UPDATE_STATS
outliers The outliers value or flag used in calculating the 
statistics. It could be one of the following:
error_code The following is a list of possible values:

6.17 DF_UPSTAT
Abstract When the Defect reader inserts raw defect data into the database, it does not 
compute wafer and lot level statistics. Instead it inserts a row in the DF_UPSTAT 
table. Furthermore, when the dbascii (ASCII) reader inserts binmap data for a bin 
program aligned with defect, a row for each wafer inserted into this table. The 
UpStat utility calculates the necessary statistics and inserts them in the DF_SUM, 
WAF... and LOT... tables.
Table
Serial Key None.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
pg_key The key of the PROGRAM to which the lot belongs.
lot_key The key to the LOT that requires defect statistics 
calculation.
wf_key The key to the WAFER that requires defect statistics 
calculation. At lot level, it is set to NULL.
lg_key A foreign key to the OP_LOG table indicating the defect 
log entry that requires statistics calculation.
step_key A foreign key to the PROC_STEP table indicating the 
key to the layer (step) that requires calculation. It is set to 
NULL, if flag = 8.
Other Fields in 
UPDATE_STATS
error_code An internal code specifying the calculation step that 
caused problems.
err_msg A descriptive text of the error message including 
Informix specific error code.

6.34 TAG_LOG
Abstract Holds information relevant to the latest tagging attempt of wafers, lots, and source 
lots.
Table
Serial Key None.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
lg_key Relates a tester summary to a logging entry in OP_LOG.
pg_key The key of the PROGRAM to which the lot belongs.
lot_key Foreign key from LOT.
wf_key The key to the WAFER that requires statistics 
recalculation. At lot level, it is set to -1.
Other Fields in 
LOT_CLASS
tag_flag contains the tagging state. The legal values are:
bad_yield *User-defined value from 0 to 100. It is used to tag the 
wafers and lots as “bad” when the yield is less than or 
equal to this value. When the value is less than 0, it is 
ignored and considered undefined. The default is -1.
scrap_yield *User-defined value from 0 to 100. It is used to tag the 
wafers and lots as “scrap” when the yield is less than or 
equal to this value. When the value is less than 0, it is 
ignored and considered undefined. The default is -1.
*When both bad_yield and scrap_yield are defined, the 
value of scrap_yield must be less than the value of 
bad_yield.

 rule_type There are two rule_types available: 
 operator The mathematical operator that was used in the 
evaluation of the rule.
 rule_value The value (integer for rule_type = N, float for rule_type = 
%) that was used in the rule evaluation.
 action The action to be taken based on the dpSSA Monitor 
result. The possible values are: 
 result The result of the rule evaluation: either Pass or Fail.

on the absolute num￾ber of wafers in the lot

 7.0 — Process Related Tables 133
dataPOWER Data Dictionary

7.0 Process Related Tables
Abstract These tables describe a Fab process.
7.1 PROC_STEP
Abstract It may be useful to distinguish different data kinds produced in one step by an 
artificial process step: e.g., if at Esort testing, both a datalog and a tester summary
sheet file are produced, which are handled as separate programs, then we may 
distinguish two steps: esort_dlog and esort_summary. In this way, we can support 
the data selection process because we are narrowing down to step related 
programs only. Also, distinguishing engineering vs. production data might be 
usefully done at a PROC_STEP level.

7.11 TOOL_OPTION
Abstract This table stores the keys of the options string used for DpLoad and for every 
DpLoad configuration line. It also stores parallel options, if used, in this table. It 
also stores -99 in func_key, since parallel options are not for a specific reader or 
tool. In this table we store keys to strings that are stored in the 
STRING_HOLDER table. The options string can be up to 1,020 characters.

I - standard inline defect program

Description
O Adder-Repeater-Missing I Defects which are both adders and 
repeaters, but also missing from the 
next layer.
R Adder - Repeater I Defects which appear at the same reti￾cle XY coordinates on multiple reticles 
(repeaters) and are also adders.
M Adder - Missing I Defects which are adders, but also 
missing from the next layer.
A Adder I A new defect that was not in any of the 
previous layers.
T Common - Repeater - 
Missing
I Defects which appear at the same 
reticle XY coordinates on multiple 
reticles (repeaters) and also on all 
layers at the same wafer XY 
coordinates (common).
S Common - Repeater I Defects which appear at the same reti￾cle XY coordinates on multiple reticles 
(repeaters) and also on all layers at the 
same wafer XY coordinates (common).
N Common - Missing I Defects which are common, but also 
missing from the next layer.
C Common I Defects that were detected in one of 
the previous layers.
K Common - In Fail Mask I Defect that were detected in one of the 
previous layers and fall into the failed 
Etest bounding box.

Abstract Contains block and multiple block set definitions, per product. Blocks are user￾defined rectangular zones of interest within die, used for monitoring defect hot￾spots. Alternatively, the table can contain size bin definitions.

Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
pd_key The key to the related product information.

owner The user who creates/defines the block or defect size bin 
set.

name Unique name for the block if type = B or D;

type = B or D;

type Four possible states:
• B — blocks
• D — default blocks. Sets user-defined blocks as auto￾matic default when defectMAP is opened.
• S — size bins.* 
• Z — default size bins. Sets user-defined size bins as 
automatic default when defectMAP is opened.*
*When type = S or Z, DF_BIN is not used.

11.0 dbTOOLS Tables
The dbTOOLS tables are all used for merging. Each row in these tables merges 
two entities of the same type — families, processes, products, or technologies.
11.1 DBTOOLS_MERGE_FAM
Abstract Used to merge two families per row. Families can be merged using this table and 
the dbTOOLS utility.
Table
Serial Key None.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description

field refers to the source family. 
dest_fam Referenced to the fm_key in the FAMILY table. This 
field refers to the destination family.

and the dbTOOLS utility.

Serial Key None.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
src_proc Referenced to the pr_key in the PROCESS table. This 
field refers to the source process.

field refers to the destination process.

A default value of parent = 0 indicates that the 
equipment has no parent.
Other Fields in 
EQUIPMENT
eq_name Name of instantiation of particular piece of equipment.
Note that EQUIPMENT does not have a link to 
DEPARTMENT or FAB, thus eq_name must be unique 
throughout the fab. Some equipment (such as Vista Vision 
and Vista Logic testers) support unique serial numbers which 
can be used as eq_name. In all other cases, a site name must 
be prepended to any site internal equipment naming scheme.
Some standardization on the usage of this field - 
especially within one site - is desirable to allow easy data 
selection.
eq_level Used to enable usage of parent to identify parent 
equipment. A value of 1 identifies parent equipment, 2 is 
a child, 3 a child of a child, etc. Default is 1. 
For Metrology and LEH programs, this field will be 
filled with 101,102 or 103:
eq_level Description
1 default (parent)
2 …100 generation level (child, child of 
child, etc.)
101 MEQUIP for metrology
102 PEQUIP for metrology and 
LEH
103 MEQUIP and PEQUIP

Absolute date/time table
Start_time End_time nlots Description
SD X X Consider all lots from start date SD till now
SD ED X Consider all lots from start date SD till end date ED
SD ED N Consider last N lots from start date SD till end date ED
SD X N Consider last N lots from start date - now
X ED X Consider all lots till end date ED
X ED N Consider last N lots till end date ED
X X N Consider last N lots
X X X See next table (Relative date/time table)
Relative date/time table
Period_value Period_type period_offset nlots Description
Val Type X X
Consider all lots for the period now - Val * Type 
(e.g. 5 * weeks)
Val Type Offset X
Consider all lots for the period (now – Val * Type) 
- Offset (Offset has the same type as Type (e.g. 
1 week)
Val Type X N
Consider last N lots for the period now – Val * 
Type (e.g. 5 * weeks)
Val Type Offset N
Consider last N lots for the period (now – Val * 
Type) - Offset (Offset has the same type as Type 
(e.g. 1 week)

 13.0 — Characterization Tables — Shmoo and Robustness 225

13.0 Characterization Tables — Shmoo and Robustness
Abstract Pattern (group of vectors) generation tools create many more patterns than 
production testers can practically use; therefore, it is necessary to select the most 
robust patterns that will most exhaustively test device sensitivity. The 
patternROBUSTNESS tool provides the means for test engineers to select the 
most robust patterns.
Shmoo plots are routinely used by development and test engineers to evaluate 
digital devices under pattern test while one or more conditions are being varied. 
The shmooPLOT tool is used for the analysis and display of shmoo data.
This section of the document contains description of the database tables that are 
used by the patternROBUSTNESS and shmooPLOT products. Those tables are 
created by the DpCreateDb.pl utility when -Char switch is provided on the 
command line.

char_cmt The comments as specified in the “Intention 2” item of

data_desc The description as specified in the “Intention 3” item of

start_time The start date and time of data collection as specified in

end_time The end date and time of data collection as specified in

insert_time The current date and time of insertion into the database. 
prog_load_date The date and time of loading the program as specified in 
“Program load date” item of the header section of the 
data log.
invalid_flag The user can mark a characterization log as invalid by 
setting this flag to '1'.
die_x The x-coordinate of a die under test as specified in “Die 
X” item of the header section of the data log.
die_y The y-coordinate of a die under test as specified in “Die 
X” item of the header section of the data log.
unit_id An identification of the unit being tested. Typically 
marked and/or scribed on the packaged part. This is 
specified in “Unit id” item of the header section of the 
data log.
fuse_id Electrically programmed fuse elements (at wafer sort) 
can be read from the device under test. This is specified 
in “Fuse id” item of the header section of the data log. 
len N-channel transistor effective channel length as specified 
in “LEN” item of the header section of the data log.
lep P-channel transistor effective channel length as specified 
in “LEP” item of the header section of the data log. 
leff An external value, if known, as specified in “LEFF” item 
of the header section of the data log.
ate_ver Software version number of the tester/ATE manufacture's 
system being exercised as specified in “ASAP version” 
item of the header section of the data log.

# Page 229

 13.0 — Characterization Tables — Shmoo and Robustness 229
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
ftemp_start Initial value of any temperature forcing device (e.g. 
wafer prober chuck, packaged part handler force 
temperature) as specified in “Force temperature” item of 
the header section of the data log.
ftemp_end Initial value of any temperature forcing device (e.g. 
wafer prober chuck, packaged part handler force 
temperature) as specified in “Force temperature” item of 
the trailer section of the data log.
mtemp_start Circuits on the device can report the die temperature 
sensed as specified in “Device temperature” item of the 
header section of the data log.
mtemp_end Circuits on the device can report the die temperature 
sensed as specified in “Device temperature” item of the 
trailer section of the data log.
vector_rev Tester pattern sets are distributed via a revision number. 
This number is critical for maintaining sanity of the test 
results gathered. This is specified in “Vector revision” 
item of the header section of the data log.
timing_block The timing waveforms associated with the given Ftest as 
specified in “Timing block” item of the header section of 
the data log.
levels_block The voltage levels associated with the given Ftest as 
specified in “Levels block” item of the header section of 
the data log.
pre_code User function written code to execute before execution of 
the Ftest. This is specified in “Pre code” item of the 
header section of the data log.
post_code User function written code to execute after execution of 
the Ftest. This is specified in “Post code” item of the 
header section of the data log.
temp_meas_flag Determines whether temperature measurements are 
taken. This is specified in “Temperature measure” item of 
the header section of the data log. When the value is 
“on”, the flag will be '1'; otherwise, the flag will be '0'.
test_pwr_down Determines whether the die or unit is powered down 
between consecutive Ftests. This is specified in “Per test 
power down” item of the header section of the data log. 
When the value is “on”, the flag will be '1'; otherwise, the 
flag will be '0'.

# Page 230

dataPOWER
230
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
pat_pwr_down Determines whether the die or unit is powered down 
between consecutive pattern tests. This is specified in 
“Per pattern power down” item of the header section of 
the data log. When the value is “on”, the flag will be '1'; 
otherwise, the flag will be '0'.
loops For the robustness data, this sets the number of “test” 
sequences to perform (e.g. 1, 2, 3). This is specified in 
“Signature sequence” item of the header section of the 
data log. 
For the shmoo data, this is the number of unique values 
of the critical parameter. In the SHMOO… and LINE…
tables, t(n) is t(loops * 3).
burst_mode When all but the first pattern in a functional test require 
only a “short” reset pulse. This is specified in “Burst 
mode” item of the header section of the data log. When 
the value is “on”, the flag will be '1'; otherwise, the flag 
will be '0'.
compare_blocks Pre-defined pins can be disabled during individual 
pattern testing. Record the fact “if any pattern” in the 
given list has a “compare block” associated with it. This 
is specified in “Compare blocks” item of the header 
section of the data log. 
dimensions Used for the shmoo data only. It holds the number of 
dimensions (number of parameters being swept) the 
shmoo data has. The “robust” reader will leave this field 
NULL.
transpose_flag Used for the shmoo data only. When it is set to 'Y', it 
means the shmoo data has been transposed by the 
“shmoo” reader along the X-axis (critical parameter 
axis). Otherwise, it is set to 'N'. The “robust” reader will 
leave this field NULL.

critical_flag ** With the shmoo data, this flag indicates whether this 
parameter is the critical parameter which will be used as 
the X-axis parameter. It is the last parameter listed in the

The value would be either 'Y' for critical parameters or 
'N' for others.
** With the robust data, this flag indicates whether the 
swept parameter has (min,max) results or (min,max,avg) 
results. The value would be '0' for (min,max) and '1' for 
(min,max,avg).

Abstract This table is used by the “robust” reader and the robustness analysis tools only. It 
contains a collection of information generated every time the pattern test result is

Description
char_key A reference to the characterization log which the fail-info 
belongs to.

Other Fields in 
ROB_FAIL
pat_index The index number of the pattern in the list of all patterns. 
This is specified in the “pattern index” item of the data 
section of the data log.
sequence When the pattern is applied multiple times, the sequence 
of tests is stored in this field. This is specified in the 
“sequence” item of the data section of the data log.
cycle This value is the actual tester cycle for a failing pattern. 
This is specified in the “tester cycle” item of the data 
section of the data log.
rma Relative Memory Address (relative to 0). This is 
specified in the “rma” item of the data section of the data 
log. 
sub If a pattern fails in a tester's subroutine memory, this is 
the absolute memory address in subroutine memory. This 
is specified in the “sub” item of the data section of the 
data log.

the data section of the data log. When sweep is “on”, this

(where n is 1 … 5) as specified in the “min” item of the 
data section of the data log.

end or not. This could be either '+' or '-' if specified after 
the min value.

13.24 PIN_PARETO
Abstract This table holds the count (number of times) a certain pin (channel) has failed per 
characterization log. It can handle up to 500 pins.
Table
Serial Key None.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
char_key A reference to the characterization log
Other Fields in 
PIN_PARETO
unique_flag For robustness data, this flag is either 'Y' or 'N'. When 
the flag is 'Y', that means the failed pin is counted only 
once per pattern. For example, if the pattern is tested five 
times and the same pin failed three times, then only one 
failure is counted for that pin. When the flag is 'N', that 
means anytime a pin fails gets counted. For example, if 
the pattern is tested five times and the same pin failed 
three times, then all three failures are counted for that 
pin. 
For shmoo data, this flag is either 'S' or 'C'. When the 
flag is 'S', that means the failed pins are counted across 
the whole characterization log. When the flag is 'C', that 
means the failed pins are counted at the contour only. The 
contour is defined as the first-fail or last-pass point of the 
parameter under test. 
p1 … p500 The count of failures per pin.
PIN_PARETO
char_key 
unique_flag 
CHAR_LOG p1 … p500 
1 (char_key) n

13.26 SHMOO…
Abstract This table is used by the “shmoo” reader and the shmoo analysis tools only. It 
contains the standard shmoo data (1,2,or 3 dimensions) where all the swept 
parameters are numeric. The table name is based on the cpar_key value of 
CRITICAL_PARAM table. For example, if the cpar_key is 5, then the table name 
would be “shmoo5”.
Table
Serial Key None.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
char_key A reference to the characterization log.
pat_key A reference to the pattern info.
t3, t6, t9, …etc. A reference to the FAIL... table.
Other Fields in 
SHMOO...
first_fail The critical parameter's value (x-axis) when the other 
parameters (y-axis, or z-axis) failed for the first time.
last_pass The critical parameter's value (x-axis) when the other 
parameters (y-axis, or z-axis) passed for the last time.
dim1 The value of the second parameter (y-axis); if available.
dim2 The value of the third parameter (z-axis); if available.
hole_flag If 'Y', that means there is at least one hole in test results. 
A hole is defined as a test failure between test passes (e.g. 
“pFp”, “ppppFFppp” have holes).
t1…tn The results are stored in a group of three (PASS_FLAG, 
TEMPERATURE, FAIL_KEY) for each value of the 
critical parameter (x-axis). Where, PASS_FLAG is 1 if 
the test passed, 0 if the test failed, and NULL if the test 
was not performed. The TEMPERATURE is the 
measured temperature whether the test passed or failed. 
The FAIL_KEY is a reference to the FAIL… table if the 
test is passed, otherwise it is NULL.
For example:

rank The rank is a number which represent the order in which 
the first fail has happened with respect to the other 
patterns. If multiple patterns have the same fail location, 
the ranking is assigned in sequence from top to bottom.

 13.0 — Characterization Tables — Shmoo and Robustness 253
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
pat3 ppFFFFFF rank = 2
pat4 pppFFFFF rank = 4
pat6 pppppppp rank = NULL
hole_flag If 'Y', that means there is at least one hole in test results. 
A hole is defined as a test failure between test passes (e.g. 
“pFp”, “ppppFFppp” have holes).
t1…tn The results are stored in a group of three (PASS_FLAG, 
TEMPERATURE, FAIL_KEY) for each value of the 
critical parameter (x-axis). Where, PASS_FLAG is 1 if 
the test passed, 0 if the test failed, and NULL if the test 
was not performed. The TEMPERATURE is the 
measured temperature whether the test passed or failed. 
The FAIL_KEY is a reference to the FAIL… table if the 
test is passed, otherwise it is NULL.
For example:
t1 t2 t3 t4 t5 t6
------- ------- ------- ------- ------- -------
1 13.6 (NULL) 0 20.4 123
for the first critical parameter value, the test passed 
where the temperature was 13.6. For the second critical 
parameter value, the test failed where the temperature 
was 20.4 and the fail_key value is 123.

13.28 FAIL…
Abstract This table is used by the “shmoo” reader and the shmoo analysis tools only. It 
contains the data produced when a test has failed. The table name is based on the 
cpar_key value of CRITICAL_PARAM table. For example, if the cpar_key is 20, 
then the table name would be “fail20”.
Table
Serial Key fail_key Automatically generated serial number.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
pg_key A reference to the program table.
char_key A reference to the characterization log.
pat_key A reference to the pattern info.
Other Fields in FAIL fail_type Either 'C' or 'F'. When it is 'C' it means the failure 
happened at the contour line. When it is 'F', it means the 
failure happened anywhere else. 
dim1 The value of the first parameter (x-axis).
dim2 The value of the second parameter (y-axis); if available 
(2D or 3D shmoo).
dim3 The value of the third parameter (z-axis); if available (3D 
shmoo).
tester_cycle This value is the actual tester cycle for a failing pattern. 
This is specified in the “tester cycle” item of the data 
section of the data log.
rma Relative Memory Address (relative to 0). This is 
specified in the “rma” item of the data section of the data 
log.

14.0 Lot Genealogy Tables

the different genealogy events (i.e. merge, split, and/or rename) that each lot has

15.0 bitMAP Tables
bitMAP tables are used to store raw bit information. These tables describe the 
portion of the schema that holds the bitMAP fail bit pattern data, and related fail 
pattern definitions and templates.
15.1 MEM_META_CLASS
Abstract MEM_META_CLASS contains meta class definitions for various bit failure 
patterns, for the purpose of broadly classifying memory cell failing patterns. 
MEM_META_CLASS is more general than MEM_CLASS, representing a family 
of memory pattern fail classes. MEM_META_CLASS is created and filled by 
DpCreateDb.pl, not by the reader. This table is part of the bitMAP application 
logic, and contents should not be modified.
Table
Serial Key mmc_key Formed from a unique mmc_name.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
None.
Other Fields in 
MEM_META_CLASS
mmc_name Memory meta class name, unique within the database.
comp_flag Indicates if the fail pattern is composite or not. 
Y = composite meta class; N = not a composite.
tf_flag Reserved for future use (column twisting).
mind_flag Indicates if min_density column in the MEM_CLASS 
table is used by classes of that meta class. 
maxd_flag Indicates if max_density column in the MEM_CLASS 
table is used by classes of that meta class.

15.2 MEM_CLASS 
Abstract MEM_CLASS contains predefined memory cell fail pattern classes — class defi￾nitions for various bit failure patterns, for the purpose of classifying memory cell 
failing patterns. A set of predefined rows of MEM_CLASS is created and filled by 
DpCreateDb.pl, not by the reader. Additional classes are created by the configura￾tion GUI.
For example, the meta class, Row, includes the memory classes Row1 and 
Row1Par. So Row1 and Row1Par are two different memory classes that belong to 
the same family — Row. Each memory class has its own properties that can be 
found in the MEM_CLASS table. However, two different memory classes can 
inherit common behavior from the meta class that both of them belong to. For 
example: both MEM_CLASS and MEM_META_CLASS contain the bitg_flag 
field. This means that for all memory classes in MEM_CLASS that belong to the 
same family or the same meta class, this bitg_flag field is only valid if bitg_flag
is equal to Y in the meta class.

max_density Maximum fail bit density required for pattern matching.
row_thickness For spot meta-class, number of rows which a given 
pattern contains. Otherwise, it means minimum number 
of rows.
col_thickness For spot meta-class, number of columns which a given 
pattern contains. Otherwise, it means minimum number 
of columns.
max_col_count For a given fail class, the maximum number of columns 
which a given pattern contains.
max_row_count For a given fail class, the maximum number of rows 
which a given pattern contains.
border_width Minimum required border width around the pattern, in 
bits, within which it is required that no failed bits be 
present.
row_flag Indicates parity of the starting row origin of the pattern: 
0 = all, 1 = odd, 2 = even.
col_flag Indicates parity of the starting column origin of the 
pattern: 0 = all, 1 = odd, 2 = even.
bitg_flag Bit groups flag. For box meta-class, indicates whether or 
not bit groups are used for pattern matching. Y = yes; N = 
no.
fail_bit_count For spot patterns, it stores the number of failing bits.Valid 
if patt_key is not null.

config_key From CV_CONFIG table. Identifies CV configuration 
entry.
zone_key From ZONES table.
fltr_key From CV_FLTR table.
Other Fields in 
CV_FRM_RUN
frm_rn_name The name the run, specified by the user in the GUI.
frm_rn_desc The description for the run, specified by the user.
insert_time Time of run insertion into the database, defaults to the 
current time.
end_time End time of run insertion into the database.
fail_style Fail style: H = hard; S = soft.
 aggregate Fail count format: S = structure; D = die.
agg_level Aggregation level of the data, as defined in the GUI. 
Four-character string: LDWZ — L = lot, D = die, W = 
wafer, Z = zone. For each character 1 = on, 0 = off.
status Status of the run.
apply_cluster_flag Whether or not user wants to apply cluster, as defined in 
the GUI. Y = yes, N = no.
min_prob Minimum probability for clustering, as defined by the 
user in the GUI.
min_count Minimum fail count for clustering, as defined by the user 
in the GUI.
crit_bad_die_pct Critical bad die percentage, as defined by the user in the 
GUI.
cluster_alg_type Cluster algorithm type for clustering. Currently hard￾coded to 1, only.
all_cluster_flag Y = all wafers are clustered; N = otherwise.
Value Description
-1 error
0 new
1 in progress
2 done

19.2 EV_...
Abstract A table will be created dynamically for every index that the user asks to be 
normalized using the built-in function NormalizeIndex.
The table name is equivalent to the index name prefixed with “EV_...”. For 
example, if FAB is declared as index and then the user asks to normalize it, a table 
is created with the name “EV_FAB”. The “…” in the table name and columns is 
replaced by the name of the index. 
Table
Serial Key …_key A unique serial key, automatically generated to identify 
each unique value of the index.
Other Fields in EV_... …_val Value of the index.
…_cnt Holds the count of the index history.
EV_... table has a link to one of the index columns (i's) in 
RES... table depending on the choice of the user. Whenever 
the user normalizes an index in RES..., an EV_... table is 
created for that index and a link to RES... is established. The 
'x' in (ix) at the link represents the number of the index at 
RES...
EV_...
..._key
.._val
..._cnt RES…
1 (ix) n

exception results generated by Tool-to-Tool analyses done in the background.

21.0 Data Mining Tables
The Data Mining tables are only used by the dataPOWER Data Mining 
classification trees. They are used to store the tree model generated by the CT 
classification tree module.
21.1 CT_JOB
Abstract This table contains references to a set of classification trees containing rules 
distinguishing a common set of data into a common set of classes.
Table
Serial Key job_key A unique key that identifies a specific classification job.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
rule_set_key A key referencing a record in the CT_RULE_SET table.
dt_key A key referencing a record in the DATA_SET table.
grp_key A key referencing a record in the PAR_GRP table.
Other Fields in 
CT_JOB
owner The name of the owner. 
class_option The action the system takes when there are duplicate 
class values. 1 = First class, 2 = Last class, 3 = Ignore.

# Page 452

dataPOWER

dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
subsample_method Defines the method in which the subsample is extracted:
random = randomly; first = extracted from the beginning 
of the data set; last = extracted from the end of the data 
set; spaced = evenly spaced throughout the data set.
max_depth The (user-defined) maximum allowed number of levels 
in the tree.
max_terminals The (user-defined) maximum allowed number of 
terminal nodes.
min_child_size The (user-defined) minimum number of records allowed 
in each node before it can be created.
min_child_method The (user-defined) method for defining the minimum 
number of records allowed in each node before it can be 
created:
fraction = % of parent; records = number of readings.
splitter_method The (user-defined) method for defining Splitter Method 
— splitter rule methodology. In this release, there are no 
options other than the default, Gini.
num_competitors An integer that defines the maximum number of 
parameters that can be used to choose the splitter 
parameter. 
num_cat_srgts An integer that defines the maximum number of 
surrogate rules that can be used to replace a categorical 
splitter parameter if it is missing.
num_cont_srgts An integer that defines the maximum number of 
surrogate rules that can be used to replace a continuous 
splitter parameter if it is missing.
surrogate_imp The weight on surrogate improvements.
missing_factor_cut The proportion of missing records that are allowed in a 
explanation variable, below which this variable is not 
considered.
rec_weighting The method used to modify the weightings of the input 
records: unit — none; equal class — such that each 
class has an equal number of records; advanced — uses 
an advanced algorithm to determine the weighting.
pruning_conf Controls the extent of the tree pruning. The smaller the 
number the larger the tree.

 24.0 — Spatial Signature Analysis Tables 461
dataPOWER Data Dictionary

24.0 Spatial Signature Analysis Tables
The purpose of Spatial Signature Analysis (SSA) is to enable the user to submit 
batch Fail Signature Detection and Analysis (FSDA) and Fail Signature 
Classification (FSC) jobs via a GUI, accessible from dataPOWER. The user will 
be able to monitor the progress of the run(s), including when the run has 
completed. 
The user submits a job via the GUI by inserting it into certain database tables, 
including SSA_RUN. The program queries the SSA_RUN table for the "status", 
"error_code" and "err_msg" fields; if status = 0, error_code = null, and err_msg = 
null, then the entry is considered a new job and the program proceeds.
SSA stores the output data into a set of database tables; it also creates the images 
and stores them in the SSA_IMAGE table:
• SSA_DIE_<pg_key>
•Tn columns - die-level statistic (mean, median, etc.) aggregated at the clus￾ter level for each cluster identified by FSDA. One column per selected 
parameter (# of selected parameters = n).
•Cn columns - # of die containing data for the selected parameter.
• SSA_ZONE_<pg_key>
•Tn columns - zone-level statistic (mean, median, etc.) aggregated at the 
wafer level for each cluster identified by FSDA. One column per selected 
parameter (# of selected parameters = n).
•Cn columns - # of die in each wafer/zone containing data for the selected 
parameter.
• SSA_CLUSTER_<pg_key>
•Tn columns - zone-level statistic (mean, median, etc.) aggregated at the 
cluster level for each cluster identified by FSDA. One column per selected 
parameter(# of selected parameters = n)
•Cn columns - # of die in each cluster/zone containing data for the selected 
parameter.
SSA stores data into other tables and also create the images and store them into 
ssa_image.

# Page 479

 — Central Schema — Overview 479
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
Central Schema — Overview
A dataPOWER central schema is an optional schema that can be generated using 
the -central function in DpCreateDb.pl. It has the capability to store information 
at database-engine level as opposed to a data-schema level. Things such as 
workflows, load balancing configuration, and dpMonitor information are among a 
few of these data storage categories.
When creating a standard data dataPOWER schema, there is an prompt to attach 
that schema to a central schema. All dataPOWER database schemas on one 
engine can be attached to one central schema on the same engine.
The following sections describe tables that can exist in both the standard data 
schema and a central schema, and also tables that exist only in the central schema.

# Page 480

25.0 Central Schema or General Schema — File Storage 
Tables
The File Storage tables in this section are always present if a Central Schema is 
created. But they can also be created when a normal dataPOWER schema is 
generated, if the -file_store command-line argument is used with DpCreateDb.pl. 
This section describes how files, folders and their attributes will be stored in the 
database. 
25.1 DP_FOLDER
Abstract This table contains information for folders stored in the database.
Table
Serial Key folder_key A unique key that will be assigned to each entry in 
DP_FOLDER table. It is stored automatically.
Foreign Keys / Link 
Description
parent_folder_key Reference to folder_key in the same table, where each 
folder should have a Parent Folder, except the root will 
be -1.
em_key Foreign key from PEOPLE table.
Other Fields in 
DP_FOLDER
folder_name The name of the folder saved in the database.
folder_type An integer, it identifies which tool created the folder:
insert_time The time of insertion of the file into the database. Date/
time/year to the second; default is current year to the 
second.
active_flag Flag indicating if setup is active (Y/N).

 29.0 — Glossary 517
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
29.0 Glossary
batch A batch (or run) is in most cases identical to a lot. However, a batch is subjected to 
common treatment in a single process step. This becomes important in a fab envi￾ronment, where some equipment can process multiple lots (or less than one lot) at 
once.
For example, some furnaces exist which can process up to 150 wafers. With a nor￾mal lot size of 25 wafers, this corresponds to six lots. Because wafers are kept in 
their respective pods, traceability remains guaranteed despite lot merging. In the 
database, the data from this furnace would result in six entries in OP_LOG with a 
common run_id.
As another example, some implanters can only handle 17 wafers at once (less than 
a full lot of 25 wafers). However, they work such that they expect a flow of lots at 
25 wafers each at the input and deliver them in the same order at the output. data￾POWER does not to handle batch composition at a sub-lot level as would be 
needed in this example.
bin A tester or wafer sorter sorts devices into multiple bins. 
Pass Bin devices pass all tests. For grading purposes, multiple pass bins may 
exist, associated to different test limits.
Fail Bin devices do not fall into any pass bin, thus fails some test(s).
Hard Bin information offered from the tester to the prober or handler.
Operator Bin as indicated to the operator on a bin box or through some other 
visual indication.
Soft Bin set by the test program (and typically reported on the tester summary). 
This is often also referred to as test counter information.
Each test typically increments a separate counter when failing. If the same test is 
executed on multiple device pins (such as output drive current measurements) typ￾ically the same counter is used.
Test Numbers on some test system have an even finer gradation than soft bins 
and refer to individual measurements.
DBA Database administrator, whose task is split into three major parts:
• guaranteeing the consistency of the data in dataPOWER by adequately config￾uring the database and the data readers.
• maintaining the size of the database by implementing appropriate backup strate￾gies.
• tuning the performance of the database using standard Informix tools.
datalog A raw data file containing device data log information as produced by a tester.

# Page 518

dataPOWER
518
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
equipment A piece of hardware on which a particular manufacturing step is executed. Each 
step requires one or more kinds of equipment (such as a tester plus a prober plus a 
probecard for a particular product, etc.). 
Each kind of equipment may exist in multiple instantiations (such as different 
identical probe cards for a given product, etc.)
Equipment is dealt with in EQUIPMENT (instantiations) and EQUIP_TYPE.
Esort Wafer testing, also called pretest.
event An action which affects equipment used for a manufacturing process or manufac￾tured goods (wafers, lots, batches). 
Events are logged in EVENT_LOG.
Fab Wafer fabrication (as opposed to test centers).
lot A lot is a collection of manufacturing goods which are subjected to a sequence of 
identical processing. Lots are traced in the LOT table. (See batch for a definition 
of the difference between a lot and a batch.) 
lot splitting Occurs when a lot is split into two or more parts, each of which is subsequently 
subjected to different processing steps or to the same steps at a different time. Lot 
splitting typically occurs for final test: e.g. a lot consisting of 24 or 50 wafers is 
split and tested into smaller, more easily tested lots.
lot merging Occurs when two or more previously unrelated lots (or lots which have been gen￾erated from a single lot by lot splitting) are merged and subjected to further com￾mon treatment. 
lot renaming Occurs if the same lot is renamed as it proceeds through the manufacturing pro￾cess. 
PCM Process Control Module - an area on the wafer with structures allowing the mea￾surement of performance of fab processes. 
Formerly, PCMs were in dedicated die locations on the wafer. However in modern 
processes, PCMs are also integrated in the scribe lines — thus there are many 
more PCMs than are actually tested in normal production. Also, the relation to a 
physical location becomes slightly less obvious.
process A fabrication process for an IC. A given process technology consists of multiple 
technical stages which may be identical for different technologies. A technical 
stage consists of different flow stages (such as cleaning, implanting, etc.) which 
are subdivided into process steps, maintained in PROC_STEP.
Esort and final test are special process steps, independent of process technology.

# Page 519

 29.0 — Glossary 519
dataPOWER Data Dictionary
Front Contents Index
product A product (in the context of dataPOWER) is a die type. It does not distinguish 
between different packaging variants or speed grades (as derived from final test￾ing).
Products are maintained in PRODUCT. Package variants are controlled through 
PKG.
program A unique output file format on a per-product basis, which always contains an 
identical set of information.
The file format per program is unique in that:
• it adheres to semantic rules unique to the equipment on which the raw data file 
is produced.
• it contains a given defined set of parameters which is measured on the product.
• it uses a predefined set of limits for the measured parameters.
A program is closely related to a test program (executed on a tester) or a recipe
(executed on fab equipment). Programs are maintained in PROGRAM.
raw data Data as produced by the equipment (or after a pre-processing step not visible to 
dataPOWER).
reader Regularly executed software which inserts raw data into the database.
recipe A recipe is a program executed on fab equipment. It corresponds to a test program 
executed on a tester.
reticle In its narrowest definition, a reticle refers to a diffusion mask, which typically 
specifies the size of multiple devices (on a wafer). This definition applies, for 
example, when doing reticle based zonal analysis on a wafer map.
Often however, the term is more loosely used to refer to a diffusion mask type, 
and thus a reticle set to a die type or product. Reticles and reticle sets (as in masks 
and mask sets) are maintained in RETICLE and RETICLE_SET.
serial key A unique key automatically generated by the Informix database engine. For tables 
that use them, the serial key is a unique identifier for the row and is generated for 
each row inserted in the table.
test Esort and final test (F/T) operation, executed on wafers or on packaged devices.
test stage Broad test category, such as Esort and Final Test (F/T). In some cases, a test stage 
also indicates the test temperature (such as WR for “wafer room”).
tester summary Summary produced by the test system. It contains counter and/or binning infor￾mation.

traceability Forward traceability ensures that when a problem is discovered at an early pro￾cessing step, all products affected by the problem can be isolated in the manufac￾turing chain or potentially at the receiving customer.
Backward traceability ensures that for bad products, all previous manufacturing 
steps can be clearly identified and thus analyzed. Backward traceability becomes 
more difficult with lot merging.
